--- Page 1 ---
This image shows the front cover of a leather-bound book featuring a central gilt-stamped armorial emblem (supra-libros). The emblem represents the royal arms of King George III and Queen Charlotte of the United Kingdom.

The text within the emblem is primarily in Old French (the traditional mottos of the British monarchy), with a Latin monogram in the center.

[Front Cover]

[Central gilt emblem consisting of two shields under a single crown, surrounded by a laurel wreath. The left shield is encircled by a garter with a motto, and the right shield is encircled by a floral wreath. A small monogrammed shield is at the center, and a ribbon with a motto is at the bottom.]

**Transcription of text within the emblem:**

[On the garter encircling the left shield:]
HONY · SOIT · QVI · MAL · Y · PENSE

[In the central small shield:]
G R

[On the ribbon at the bottom of the emblem:]
[unclear] ET MON [unclear]

---
*Note: The motto "HONY SOIT QVI MAL Y PENSE" is Old French for "Shame be to him who thinks evil of it." The monogram "G R" stands for the Latin "Georgius Rex" (King George). The faint motto at the bottom is "DIEU ET MON DROIT," which is French for "God and my right."*

The 'A' is a woodcut initial.
`Agrippina Marci agrippe`
`filia ex Agrippina vxore su`
`cepta Neronisqꝫ mat / post`
`christi natiuitatē āno quin`
`quagesimo ⁊ genere psāguinitateqꝫ / ⁊`
`filij mōstruositate / ac nō minus suay qꝫ`
`vllis claris facinorib⁹ / iter claras mlie`
`res pputata fuit.`
Wait, `pputata` is `reputata`. The first letter is an 'r'. Let's compare it with other 'r's.
Look at `res` right before it. The 'r' is different.
Look at `Agrippina`. The 'r' is different.
Look at `genere`. The 'r' is different.
The letter in `pputata` is a 'p' with a bar.
Maybe it's `reputata` and the 'r' is just weird? No, it's a 'p'.
Could it be `perputata`?
Actually, `reputata` is the standard word here.
Let's look at the 'r' in `res`. It's a 'round r' (like a 2) because it follows an 'e'.

veneno illitis pēnis/ ad vomituʒ ꝓtinuādū poꝛrectis/ eo ituz ē/q̄ cupie"
"pēnis" -> "pennis".
"vomituʒ" -> "vomitum".
"ꝓtinuādū" -> "procontinuandum"? No, "continuandum". The 'p' with a stroke is 'pro'.
Actually, it's "ꝓtinuādū".
"ituz ē" -> "itum est".
"q̄" -> "quam".
Line 29: "bat vxoꝛ: Jp̄e tādē i cubiculuz reduct⁹ ignaris oib⁹/ p̄ter Agrippinā moꝛtu⁹"
"vxoꝛ" -> "uxor".
"tādē" -> "tandem".
"reduct⁹" -> "reductus".
"oib⁹" -> "omnibus".
"p̄ter" -> "praeter".
"moꝛtu⁹" -> "mortuus".
Line 30: "ē. Cui⁹ qdeʒ moꝛs nō āte ab Agrippia palā nūtiata ē/q̄ʒ amicoꝛum ſuffragia"
"ē" -> "est".
"qdeʒ" -> "quidem".
"moꝛs" -> "mors".

De Sancta Monica Et Sancta Fabiola
O quantum tibi nos omnes debere fatemur:
Cuius ope est mundo tanta reddita salus:
Quippe nisi e tenebris ad lumina sacra redisset.
Conuersus meritis filius ipse tuis.
Altiloquas totum nisi diffudisset in orbem
Doctrinas: tantum totq; voluminibus.
Errorum nisi tot pestes strauisset iniquas
Ingenio nisi tot dogmata praua suo
Ecclesiam tanto que firmauere ruuentem
Robore: felices o lachryme atque preces:
O dulces lachryme vincantq; mella fauosq;
Que vincant dulces 7 omne preces:
Quid mirum si nostra fides cultusq; perisset:
Si totus noster religionis honos.
Nunc vero superi quando ad fastigia celi.
Euictam nunc te purior orbis habet:
Quando hac terrena de fece 7 carcere trñ
Eruta: tu loca nunc liberiora colis:
Nunc nostris melius rogitans tu c̃sule rebus
Pro nostra melius funde salute preces:
Affer opem nunc tu mundo succurre labenti
Succurre afflictis rebus 7 affer opem:
Te cuncte exorant gentes / tibi vota profundunt.
Succurre 7 cunctis gentibus affer opem:
Omnes te serus / omnisq; te inuocat etas.
Te quicunq; vocent / omnibus affer opem:
Precipue matres quantum tu experta labores
Et natis: 7 tu matribus affer opem:
Te docti ante omnes / ne tu despice doctos
Nemo tuo nato doctior affer opem:
Salue lux matrum / mater sanctissima salue
O salue eterna laude canẽda parens: Amen.
